![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Javanese_ha.svg/40px-Javanese_ha.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)

**This article contains letters from the [Javanese script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_script "Javanese script").** Without proper [rendering support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Multilingual_support#Javanese "Help:Multilingual support"), you may see [question marks, boxes, or other symbols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specials_(Unicode_block)#Replacement_character "Specials (Unicode block)") instead of Javanese characters.

**Majapahit** ( [Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_language "Javanese language"): ꦩꦗꦥꦲꦶꦠ꧀, romanized:_Måjåpahit_; Javanese pronunciation:[\[mɔd͡ʒɔpaɪt\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA "Help:IPA")(eastern and central dialect) or \[&#8202;](#ref-back-9-0)[mad͡ʒapaɪt\](western dialect)), also known as **Wilwatikta**[\[note 5\]](#refnote-9) ( [Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_language "Javanese language"): ꦮꦶꦭ꧀ꦮꦠꦶꦏ꧀ꦠ; Javanese pronunciation:[\[wɪlwatɪkta\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA "Help:IPA"), [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit_language "Sanskrit language"): विल्वतिक्त, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Sanskrit "Romanization of Sanskrit"):_vilvatikta_), was a [Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_people "Javanese people") [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism "Hinduism")- [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism "Buddhism") [thalassocratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thalassocracy "Thalassocracy") empire in [Southeast Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asia "Southeast Asia") based on the island of [Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java "Java") (in modern-day [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia "Indonesia")).[&#8202;](#ref-back-10-0)[\[5\]](#refnote-10) At its greatest extent, following significant military expansions, the territory of the empire and its tributary states covered almost the entire [Nusantara archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusantara_(term) "Nusantara (term)"), spanning both [Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia "Asia") and [Oceania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceania "Oceania").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Robson_Desawarnana-0)[\[6\]](#refnote-Robson_Desawarnana)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Cribb-0)[\[7\]](#refnote-Cribb)[&#8202;](#ref-back-web.archive.org-0)[\[8\]](#refnote-web.archive.org) After a [civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regreg_war "Regreg war") that weakened control over the vassal states, the empire slowly declined before collapsing in 1527 due to an [invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak%E2%80%93Majapahit_conflicts "Demak–Majapahit conflicts") by the [Sultanate of Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak_Sultanate "Demak Sultanate"). The fall of Majapahit saw the rise of [Islamic kingdoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Indonesia#Islamic_civilizations "History of Indonesia") in Java.

Established by [Raden Wijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Wijaya "Raden Wijaya") in 1292, Majapahit rose to power after the [Mongol invasion of Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasion_of_Java "Mongol invasion of Java") and reached its peak during the era of the queen [Tribhuvana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribhuwana_Wijayatunggadewi "Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi") and her son [Hayam Wuruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayam_Wuruk "Hayam Wuruk"), whose reigns in the mid-14th century were marked by conquests that extended throughout Southeast Asia. This achievement is also credited to the famous prime minister Patih [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada"). According to the [_Nagarakṛtāgama_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama") written in 1365, Majapahit was an empire of 98 tributaries, stretching from [Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumatra "Sumatra") to [New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guinea "New Guinea");[&#8202;](#ref-back-Robson_Desawarnana-1)[\[6\]](#refnote-Robson_Desawarnana)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Cribb-1)[\[7\]](#refnote-Cribb):87[&#8202;](#ref-back-web.archive.org-1)[\[8\]](#refnote-web.archive.org) including territories in present-day [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia "Indonesia"), [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore "Singapore"), [Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia "Malaysia"), [Brunei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brunei "Brunei"), southern [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand "Thailand"), [Timor Leste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timor_Leste "Timor Leste"), and southwestern [Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines "Philippines") (in particular the [Sulu Archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulu_Archipelago "Sulu Archipelago")), although the scope of Majapahit sphere of influence is still the subject of debate among historians.[&#8202;](#ref-back-M_Wood-0)[\[9\]](#refnote-M_Wood)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Britannica-Majapahit-0)[\[10\]](#refnote-Britannica-Majapahit) The nature of Majapahit's relations and influence upon its overseas vassals and also its status as an empire still provokes discussion.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sastrawan_Empire-1)[\[1\]](#refnote-Sastrawan_Empire)

Majapahit was one of the last major Hindu-Buddhist empires of the region and is considered to be one of the greatest and most powerful empires in the history of Indonesia and Southeast Asia. It is sometimes seen as the precedent for Indonesia's modern boundaries.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-0)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):19[&#8202;](#ref-back-JPMajapahit-0)[\[12\]](#refnote-JPMajapahit) Its influence extended beyond the modern territory of Indonesia and has been the subject of many studies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-18-0)[\[13\]](#refnote-18)[&#8202;](#ref-back-19-0)[\[14\]](#refnote-19)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Muljana_Puncak-0)[\[15\]](#refnote-Muljana_Puncak)[&#8202;](#ref-back-21-0)[\[16\]](#refnote-21)

## Etymology

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Etymology")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5c/011_Mojo_Fruit%2C_Candi_Brahu_%2839533866225%29.jpg/250px-011_Mojo_Fruit%2C_Candi_Brahu_%2839533866225%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:011_Mojo_Fruit,_Candi_Brahu_(39533866225).jpg) A _[maja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegle_marmelos "Aegle marmelos")_ fruit growing near Trowulan. The bitter-tasting fruit is the origin of the kingdom's name.

The name _Majapahit_ (sometimes also spelled _Mojopait_ to reflect Javanese pronunciation), derives from [Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_language "Javanese language"), meaning "bitter _maja_". German [orientalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orientalism#Oriental_studies "Orientalism") [Berthold Laufer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berthold_Laufer "Berthold Laufer") suggested that the _maja_ element comes from the Javanese name of _[Aegle marmelos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aegle_marmelos "Aegle marmelos")_, an Indonesian tree.[&#8202;](#ref-back-22-0)[\[17\]](#refnote-22) Majapahit is also referred to by the Sanskrit-derived synonym _Wilwatikta_ ( [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit_language "Sanskrit language"): विल्वतिक्त, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Sanskrit "Romanization of Sanskrit"):_vilvatikta_, [lit.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literal_translation "Literal translation")'bitter _maja_'). Toponyms containing the word _maja_ are common in the area in and around [Trowulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan "Trowulan") (e.g. [Mojokerto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mojokerto "Mojokerto")), as it is a widespread practice in Java to name an area, a village or settlement with the most conspicuous or abundant tree or fruit species found in that region. The 16th-century chronicle _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_ records a legend linked to the establishment of a new settlement in the forest of Trik by [Raden Wijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Wijaya "Raden Wijaya") in 1292.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Pararaton_Brandes-0)[\[18\]](#refnote-Pararaton_Brandes):23,74,92 It was said that the workers clearing the Trik forest encountered some _maja_ trees and consumed their bitter-tasting fruit which then gave its name to the village.[&#8202;](#ref-back-NGI_Trowulan-1)[\[3\]](#refnote-NGI_Trowulan)

Strictly speaking, the name _Majapahit_ refers to the kingdom's capital, but today it is common to refer to the kingdom by its capital's name. In Javanese primary sources, the kingdom as an extended territory is generally referred to not as _Majapahit_ but rather as _bhūmi Jawa_ ("land of [Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java "Java")") in [Old Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Javanese "Old Javanese") or _yava-dvīpa- [maṇḍala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandala_(political_model) "Mandala (political model)")_ ("country of the island of Java") in [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit").

## Historiography

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Historiography")\]

### Archaeological evidence

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: Archaeological evidence")\]

Compared to contemporary societies elsewhere in Asia, little physical evidence of Majapahit remains.[&#8202;](#ref-back-24-0)[\[19\]](#refnote-24) However, Majapahit did produce physical evidence: the main ruins dating from the Majapahit period are clustered in the [Trowulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan "Trowulan") area, which was the royal capital of the kingdom. The area has become the centre for the study of Majapahit history. The Trowulan archaeological site was first documented in the 19th century by [Sir Thomas Stamford Raffles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stamford_Raffles "Stamford Raffles"), Lieutenant-Governor of [British Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_and_British_interregnum_in_the_Dutch_East_Indies#British_interregnum_1811%E2%80%931815 "French and British interregnum in the Dutch East Indies") of the [East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_India_Company "East India Company") from 1811 to 1816. He reported the existence of "ruins of temples... scattered about the country for many miles", and referred to Trowulan as "this pride of Java".[&#8202;](#ref-back-25-0)[\[20\]](#refnote-25) Aerial and satellite imagery has revealed an extensive network of canals crisscrossing the Majapahit capital.[&#8202;](#ref-back-26-0)[\[21\]](#refnote-26) Findings from April 2011 indicate the Majapahit capital was much larger than previously believed after some artefacts were uncovered.[&#8202;](#ref-back-27-0)[\[22\]](#refnote-27)

### Inscriptions

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Inscriptions")\]

While significant details about the history of Majapahit remain vague,[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-1)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):18 this period of Javanese history is the more comprehensively documented than any other. The most reliable written sources for this period are [Old Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Javanese "Old Javanese") inscriptions on stone and metal, which are contemporary with the events they describe. These inscriptions provide valuable information about dynasties, religious affairs, village communities, society, economics, and the arts.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Boechari_2012-0)[\[23\]](#refnote-Boechari_2012):255–236

The Majapahit dynasty is described in royal inscriptions such as Kudadu (issued in 1294), Sukhamerta (1296), Tuhanyaru (1323), Gajah Mada (1351), Waringin Pitu (1447) and Trailokyapuri (1486). These sources help us to clarify the family relationships and chronologies of the Majapahit rulers, as well as to correct certain errors found in the _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_. A few inscriptions found outside Java, such as the Pura Abang C inscription discovered in northern Bali, offer conclusive evidence that these areas were under Majapahit control during the late 14th century.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sastrawan_Empire-2)[\[1\]](#refnote-Sastrawan_Empire)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Nagarakertagama.jpg/250px-Nagarakertagama.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Nagarakertagama.jpg)_[''Deśarvarṇana''](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_ [palm-leaf manuscript](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palm-leaf_manuscript "Palm-leaf manuscript") in the National Library of Indonesia collection.

### Javanese chronicles

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Javanese chronicles")\]

Two important chronicle sources are available to historians of Majapahit: _[Deśavarṇana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_ ("Description of Districts") was composed in 1365 and the _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_ ("The Monarchs") was compiled sometime between 1481 and 1600.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Johns1964-0)[\[24\]](#refnote-Johns1964) Both of these chronicles survive as 19th- or 20th-century palm-leaf manuscripts. The _Deśavarṇana_ (also known as _Nagarakretagama_) is an [Old Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Javanese "Old Javanese") [eulogy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eulogy "Eulogy") written during the Majapahit golden age under the reign of [Hayam Wuruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayam_Wuruk "Hayam Wuruk"), after which some events are covered narratively.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-2)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):18 Composed by [Mpu Prapanca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mpu_Prapanca "Mpu Prapanca"), it provides a primary historical account of Majapahit court during the reign of King [Hayam Wuruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayam_Wuruk "Hayam Wuruk"), as well as detailed information about the East Javanese countryside and a summary of [Singhasari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singhasari "Singhasari") history. The _Pararaton_ focuses on [Ken Arok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ken_Arok "Ken Arok"), the founder of [Singhasari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singhasari "Singhasari"), but includes a number of shorter narrative fragments about the formation of Majapahit.

The Javanese sources incorporate some poetic mythological elements into their historical accounts. This complexity has led to a variety of interpretive approaches. Cornelis Christiaan Berg, a Dutch historian, have considered the entire historical record to be not a record of the past, but a supernatural means by which the future can be determined.[&#8202;](#ref-back-31-0)[\[note 6\]](#refnote-31)[&#8202;](#ref-back-M_Wood-1)[\[9\]](#refnote-M_Wood) Most scholars do not accept this view, as the historical record corresponds in part with Chinese materials that could not have had similar intention. The references to rulers and details of the state structure show no sign of being invented.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-3)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):18

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/%E7%88%AA%E5%93%87%E8%B6%95%E8%80%85%E4%BC%AF%E5%A4%B7%E5%9C%8B%E7%8E%8B%E7%9A%AE%E5%BD%B1%E6%88%B2%E5%81%B6.jpg/250px-%E7%88%AA%E5%93%87%E8%B6%95%E8%80%85%E4%BC%AF%E5%A4%B7%E5%9C%8B%E7%8E%8B%E7%9A%AE%E5%BD%B1%E6%88%B2%E5%81%B6.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:%E7%88%AA%E5%93%87%E8%B6%95%E8%80%85%E4%BC%AF%E5%A4%B7%E5%9C%8B%E7%8E%8B%E7%9A%AE%E5%BD%B1%E6%88%B2%E5%81%B6.jpg) [Wayang klithik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayang "Wayang") puppet of Raden [Damar Wulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damarwulan "Damarwulan")–the hero of Majapahit kingdom

### Chinese sources

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Chinese sources")\]

Chinese historical sources on Majapahit mainly derive from the chronicles of the [Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty "Yuan dynasty") and following [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty "Ming dynasty"). A key source of material is from Admiral [Zheng He](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheng_He "Zheng He")'s reports during [his visit to Majapahit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_treasure_voyages "Ming treasure voyages") between 1405–1432. Zheng He's translator, [Ma Huan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma_Huan "Ma Huan"), wrote a detailed description of Majapahit and where the king of Java lived.[&#8202;](#ref-back-yingyai-0)[\[26\]](#refnote-yingyai) The report was composed and collected in his 1451 _[Yingya Shenglan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yingya_Shenglan "Yingya Shenglan")_ ("The Overall Survey of the Ocean's Shores"), which provides valuable insight on the culture, customs, and various social and economic aspects of Java (爪哇, _Zhǎowā_) during the Majapahit period.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chao-Wa-0)[\[27\]](#refnote-Chao-Wa) The [Ming Veritable Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_Veritable_Records "Ming Veritable Records") (_Ming Shilu_) provide supporting evidence for specific events in Majapahit history, such as the [Regreg War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regreg_War "Regreg War").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ming_shilu-0)[\[28\]](#refnote-Ming_shilu)

## History

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=7 "Edit section: History")\]

### Formation

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=8 "Edit section: Formation")\]

After the [Pamalayu campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pamalayu "Pamalayu") to conquer the [Melayu Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melayu_Kingdom "Melayu Kingdom") in Sumatra from 1275–1292,[&#8202;](#ref-back-35-0)[\[29\]](#refnote-35) the kingdom of [Singhasari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singhasari "Singhasari") became the most powerful polity in the region. Shortly after, Singhasari was directly challenged by Emperor [Kublai Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kublai_Khan "Kublai Khan"), the [Khagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khagan "Khagan") of the [Mongol Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_Empire "Mongol Empire") and emperor of the Mongol-led [Yuan dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty "Yuan dynasty") of China, who sent emissaries demanding tribute. The king of Singhasari, King [Kertanagara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kertanagara "Kertanagara"), refused to pay the tribute and mutilated a Yuan minister's face before sending him back to China. Enraged, Emperor Kublai Khan [sent a massive expedition of 30,000 soldiers and 1,000 ships to Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasion_of_Java "Mongol invasion of Java") as a [punitive expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punitive_expedition "Punitive expedition") in 1293.

#### Mongol invasion

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=9 "Edit section: Mongol invasion")\]

Main article: [Mongol invasion of Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasion_of_Java "Mongol invasion of Java")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Ser_Marco_Polo_Ancient_Chinese_War_Vessel.jpg/250px-Ser_Marco_Polo_Ancient_Chinese_War_Vessel.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ser_Marco_Polo_Ancient_Chinese_War_Vessel.jpg) Painting of a 14th-century [Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty "Yuan dynasty") [junk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junk_(ship) "Junk (ship)"). Similar ships were sent by the Yuan in their naval armada.

By the time that the Yuan army returned to Java to exact their revenge, however, King [Kertanagara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kertanagara "Kertanagara") was already dead; he had been killed by [Jayakatwang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayakatwang "Jayakatwang"), the _Adipati_ (Duke) of the [Kingdom of Kediri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kediri_(historical_kingdom) "Kediri (historical kingdom)"), a vassal state of Singhasari. Meanwhile, King Kertanegara's son-in-law, [Raden Wijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Wijaya "Raden Wijaya"), had been pardoned by the usurper King Jayakatwang with the aid of [Aria Wiraraja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aria_Wiraraja "Aria Wiraraja"), the Regent of [Madura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madura "Madura"), and was given timberland in [Trowulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan "Trowulan") as consolation. Raden Wijaya built a new settlement there, naming the village _Majapahit_ after a type of fruit (_maja_) growing there that had a bitter taste (_pahit_). Thus, when the Yuan army arrived, Raden Wijaya allied himself with them to fight against King Jayakatwang. Once King Jayakatwang had been defeated, Raden Wijaya launched a surprise attack and turned against his former Yuan allies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Slamet_Muljana_2005-0)[\[30\]](#refnote-Slamet_Muljana_2005) The Yuan army was forced to withdraw in confusion, as they were in hostile territory and their ships were constantly being attacked. It was also their last chance to catch the [monsoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsoon "Monsoon") winds home; otherwise, they would have had to wait for another six months.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTENugroho2011107–116-0)[\[31\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTENugroho2011107–116)

#### The first king, Kertarajasa Jayawardhana

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=10 "Edit section: The first king, Kertarajasa Jayawardhana")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Harihara_Majapahit_1.JPG/250px-Harihara_Majapahit_1.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Harihara_Majapahit_1.JPG) King [Kertarajasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kertarajasa "Kertarajasa") portrayed as [Harihara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harihara "Harihara"), amalgamation of [Shiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shiva "Shiva") and [Vishnu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vishnu "Vishnu"). Originally located at Candi Simping, [Blitar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blitar "Blitar"), today it is displayed in [National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Indonesia "National Museum of Indonesia").

In 1293, [Raden Wijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Wijaya "Raden Wijaya") founded a stronghold with the capital [Majapahit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan "Trowulan").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-0)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):200–201 The exact date used as the birth of the Majapahit kingdom is the day of his coronation, the 15th day of the month of [Kārttika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kartika_(month) "Kartika (month)") in the year 1215 of the Javanese [Śaka era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaka_era "Shaka era"), which equates to 10 November 1293.[&#8202;](#ref-back-NGI_Trowulan-2)[\[3\]](#refnote-NGI_Trowulan) During his coronation, he was given the [regnal name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regnal_name "Regnal name") Kertarajasa Jayawardhana. King Kertarajasa took the remaining three daughters of his dead father-in-law King [Kertanegara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kertanegara "Kertanegara") as his wives, with his first wife Tribhuwaneswari being prime queen consort, and her three sisters as [concubines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concubine "Concubine"): Prajnaparamita, Narendraduhita, and the youngest, [Gayatri Rajapatni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gayatri_Rajapatni "Gayatri Rajapatni"). According to the _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_ ( _Book of Kings_), he also wed a princess named [Dara Petak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dara_Petak "Dara Petak") from [Dharmasraya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dharmasraya "Dharmasraya"), the capital of the [Sumatran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumatra "Sumatra") [Melayu Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melayu_Kingdom "Melayu Kingdom").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Suhadi-0)[\[33\]](#refnote-Suhadi)[&#8202;](#ref-back-SNI-II:Zaman_Kuno-0)[\[34\]](#refnote-SNI-II:Zaman_Kuno):454

The new Majapahit kingdom faced challenges. Some of Kertarajasa's most trusted men, including Ranggalawe, Sora, and Nambi, rebelled against him, though unsuccessfully. It was suspected that the [Mahapati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicegerent "Vicegerent") Halayudha had set a conspiracy to overthrow all of his rivals in the court by enticing them to revolt against the king, while he gained king's favor and thus attained the highest position in the government. However, following the death of the last rebel Kuti, Halayudha's treachery was exposed, and he was subsequently captured, jailed, and sentenced to death.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Slamet_Muljana_2005-1)[\[30\]](#refnote-Slamet_Muljana_2005) Wijaya himself died in 1309.

#### Jayanegara

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: Jayanegara")\]

Kertarajasa Wijaya was succeeded by his heir [Jayanegara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayanegara "Jayanegara"). The reign of Jayanegara was a difficult and chaotic one, troubled with several rebellions by his father's former companions in arms. Among them were Gajah Biru's rebellion in 1314, Semi rebellion in 1318, and the Kuti rebellion in 1319. The Kuti rebellion was the most dangerous one, as Kuti managed to take control of the capital city. With the help of [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada") and his _Bhayangkara_ palace guard,[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-1)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):233 Jayanegara barely escaped from the capital and safely hid in the village of Badander. While the king was in hiding, Gajah Mada returned to the capital city to assess the situation, and after learning that Kuti's rebellion was supported neither by the common people nor by the nobles of Majapahit court, he raised resistance forces to crush the Kuti rebellion.

After Kuti forces were defeated, Jayanegara safely returned to his throne. For his loyalty and excellent service, Gajah Mada was promoted to high office to begin his career in royal court politics.[&#8202;](#ref-back-41-0)[\[35\]](#refnote-41)

According to tradition, King Jayanegara was notorious for his immorality. One of his distasteful acts was his desire to take his half-sisters, Gitarja and Rajadewi, as wives. Since Javanese tradition abhorred the practice of half-siblings marrying, the council of royal elders spoke strongly against the king's wishes. It was not clear what motivated Jayanegara's wish–it might have been his way to ensure his claim to the throne by preventing rivals from being his half-sisters' suitors, although in the later period of the Majapahit court the custom of marriage among cousins was quite common. In the _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_ ( _Book of Kings_), he was known as _Kala Gemet_, or "weak villain". Around the time of Jayanegara's reign in the early 14th century, the Italian friar [Odoric of Pordenone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odoric_of_Pordenone "Odoric of Pordenone") visited the Majapahit court in Java, noting that it was well-populated and filled with cloves, nutmeg, and many other spices. He also mentioned that the king of Java had seven vassals under him and engaged in several wars with the "khan of Cathay".[&#8202;](#ref-back-:12-0)[\[36\]](#refnote-:12):156

In 1328, King Jayanegara was murdered by his physician, Tanca, during a surgical operation. In complete mayhem and rage, Gajah Mada immediately killed Tanca. The motive behind this [regicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regicide "Regicide") was never clear. According to the _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_, it was Tanca's revenge for the king sexually abusing his wife. However, according to the Balinese manuscript _[Babad Dalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babad_Dalem "Babad Dalem")_, the assassination was a stratagem crafted by Gajah Mada himself to rid the kingdom of an evil tyrant.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Tirto-Gajah_Mada-0)[\[37\]](#refnote-Tirto-Gajah_Mada) Tradition mentions that the immoral, cruel, and abusive king often seduced and abused women, even the wives of his own subordinates.[&#8202;](#ref-back-44-0)[\[38\]](#refnote-44) Other possible reason includes to protect the two princesses—King Jayanegara's half-sisters, [Dyah Gitarja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribhuwana_Wijayatunggadewi "Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi") and Rajadewi Maharajasa, the daughters of his stepmother Queen [Gayatri Rajapatni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gayatri_Rajapatni "Gayatri Rajapatni")—from Jayanegara's cruelty.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Tirto-Gajah_Mada-1)[\[37\]](#refnote-Tirto-Gajah_Mada) Since the slain king was childless, he left no successor.

### Golden age

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Golden age")\]

#### Queen Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: Queen Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Parvati_Majapahit_2.JPG/250px-Parvati_Majapahit_2.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Parvati_Majapahit_2.JPG) A [mortuary deified portrayal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mortuary_cult "Mortuary cult") of [Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribhuwana_Wijayatunggadewi "Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi") (Dyah Gitarja), Queen of Majapahit, portrayed as the Mother Goddess [Parvati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parvati "Parvati")

King [Jayanegara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jayanegara "Jayanegara")'s stepmother, Queen Consort of Majapahit [Gayatri Rajapatni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gayatri_Rajapatni "Gayatri Rajapatni")—the most revered matriarch of the court—was set to ascend the throne. However, Rajapatni had retired from worldly affairs to become a [Buddhist nun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhikkhuni "Bhikkhuni"), and instead appointed her daughter, [Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribhuwana_Wijayatunggadewi "Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi") (Dyah Gitarja), to be queen under her auspices. Under her formal [regnal name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regnal_name "Regnal name") of Tribhuwannottunggadewi Jayawishnuwardhani, she appointed [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada") as her _[mahapatih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangkubhumi "Mangkubhumi")_ ( [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_minister "Prime minister")) in 1336. During his inauguration as _mahapatih_, Gajah Mada made his famous [Palapa oath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palapa_oath "Palapa oath"), declaring that he would not enjoy a moment's rest until he [unified the archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusantara_(term) "Nusantara (term)") under Majapahit rule.[&#8202;](#ref-back-JPMajapahit-1)[\[12\]](#refnote-JPMajapahit)

During [Queen Tribhuwana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribhuwana_Wijayatunggadewi "Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi")'s rule, Majapahit territory greatly expanded, and under the initiative of her able and ambitious prime minister, [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada"), Majapahit sent its armada to conquer the neighbouring island of [Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali "Bali").[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-2)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):234 According to the _[Babad Arya Tabanan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babad_Arya_Tabanan?action=edit&redlink=1 "Babad Arya Tabanan (page does not exist)")\[ [id](https://id.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babad%20Arya%20Tabanan "id:Babad Arya Tabanan")\]_ manuscript, in 1342, Majapahit forces led by Gajah Mada and assisted by General Arya Damar, the regent of [Palembang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palembang "Palembang"), landed in the [Bali Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali_Kingdom "Bali Kingdom"). After seven months of battles, Majapahit defeated the Balinese king and captured the Balinese capital of [Bedulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedulu "Bedulu") in 1343. After the [conquest of Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali_Kingdom#Majapahit_period "Bali Kingdom"), Majapahit distributed the governing authority of Bali among General Arya Damar's younger brothers: Arya Kenceng, Arya Kutawandira, Arya Sentong, and Arya Belog. Arya Kenceng led his brothers to govern Bali under Majapahit suzerainty, and he would become the progenitor of the Balinese kings of the [Tabanan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_monarchs_of_Bali#Rajas_of_Tabanan "List of monarchs of Bali") and [Badung royal houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_monarchs_of_Bali#Rajas_of_Pamecutan_in_Badung "List of monarchs of Bali"). Through this campaign, Majapahit planted a vassal dynasty that would rule the Bali Kingdom in the following centuries. Queen Tribhuwana ruled Majapahit until the death of her mother, Queen Consort [Gayatri Rajapatni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gayatri_Rajapatni "Gayatri Rajapatni"), in 1350, whereupon Tribhuwana abdicated the throne in favour of her son, [Hayam Wuruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayam_Wuruk "Hayam Wuruk").

[Maghrebi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maghrebis "Maghrebis") explorer [Ibn Battuta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibn_Battuta "Ibn Battuta") visited the island during his travels between 1332 and 1347, noting that he stopped by a place called _Mul Jawa_ (the "island of Java", as opposed to _al-Jawa_ which referred to [Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumatra "Sumatra")). He noted that the empire took two months to travel, and ruled over a walled city called "_Qaqula_" and a place called "_Qamara_". Upon arriving at _Qaqula_, Ibn Battuta observed that the city had war [junks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Junk_(ship) "Junk (ship)") for pirate raiding and collecting tolls, and that elephants were employed for various purposes. He met the ruler of _Mul Jawa_ and stayed as a guest for three days.[&#8202;](#ref-back-45-0)[\[39\]](#refnote-45):96–97[&#8202;](#ref-back-:Beckingham-0)[\[40\]](#refnote-:Beckingham):880–883 Ibn Battuta said that the women of Java rode horses, understood archery, and fought like men.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Gabriel-0)[\[41\]](#refnote-Gabriel) He also recorded a story about a country called __[Tawalisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tawalisi "Tawalisi")__ (also thought by some scholars to be Java), whose king opposed the Emperor of the [Yuan dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty "Yuan dynasty") in China, and waged war with the Chinese using numerous junks "until they come to terms with him on certain conditions".[&#8202;](#ref-back-Gabriel-1)[\[41\]](#refnote-Gabriel)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:Beckingham-1)[\[40\]](#refnote-:Beckingham):884–885[&#8202;](#ref-back-48-0)[\[42\]](#refnote-48)

#### Reign of Hayam Wuruk and Gajah Mada's conquest

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: Reign of Hayam Wuruk and Gajah Mada's conquest")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/87/Majapahit_Expansion.gif/330px-Majapahit_Expansion.gif?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Majapahit_Expansion.gif) Rough estimations of Majapahit's conquest of the Indonesian archipelago ( [Nusantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusantara_(archipelago) "Nusantara (archipelago)")) in the 13th century, its decline, and its eventual fall in the early 16th century. The existing historical records from several sources[&#8202;](#ref-back-49-0)[\[note 7\]](#refnote-49) only partially describe the years listed, and are thus subject to revisions.

King [Hayam Wuruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayam_Wuruk "Hayam Wuruk"), also known as Rajasanagara, ruled Majapahit from 1350–1389. During this period, Majapahit attained its peak with the help of _[mahapatih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangkubhumi "Mangkubhumi")_ (prime minister) [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada"), who maintained his power from 1313–1364. Under Gajah Mada's command, Majapahit conquered more territories and became the regional power.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-3)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):234 According to the 1365 _[Nagarakretagama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_, Canto XIII and XIV mentions several states in [Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumatra "Sumatra"), the [Malay Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_Peninsula "Malay Peninsula"), [Borneo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borneo "Borneo"), [Sulawesi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulawesi "Sulawesi"), the [Nusa Tenggara islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusa_Tenggara "Nusa Tenggara"), the [Maluku Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maluku_Islands "Maluku Islands"), [New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guinea "New Guinea"), [Mindanao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mindanao "Mindanao"), the [Sulu Archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulu_Archipelago "Sulu Archipelago"), [Luzon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luzon "Luzon"), and some parts of the [Visayas islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visayas "Visayas") as being under Majapahit rule. The _[Hikayat Raja Pasai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hikayat_Raja-raja_Pasai "Hikayat Raja-raja Pasai")_, a 14th-century [Acehenese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aceh "Aceh") chronicle, describes a Majapahit naval invasion on the [Samudera Pasai Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samudera_Pasai_Sultanate "Samudera Pasai Sultanate") on the north coast of [Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumatra "Sumatra") in 1350.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Tirto-Pasai-0)[\[43\]](#refnote-Tirto-Pasai) The attacking force consisted of four hundred large [jong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djong_(ship) "Djong (ship)") ships, and an uncountable number of [malangbang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malangbang "Malangbang") barges and [kelulus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelulus "Kelulus") rowing boats.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-0)[\[44\]](#refnote-:2) This expansion marked the greatest extent of Majapahit, making it one of the most influential empires in Indonesian history.

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/Gajah-Mada.jpg/250px-Gajah-Mada.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gajah-Mada.jpg) A [terracotta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terracotta "Terracotta") figure popularly believed by [Minister of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Culture_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Education and Culture (Indonesia)") and [national hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Hero_of_Indonesia "National Hero of Indonesia") [Mohammad Yamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Yamin "Mohammad Yamin") to be a portrait of [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada"), collection of the [Trowulan Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan_Museum "Trowulan Museum"). His claim, however, is not backed by historical sources.

In 1355, Hayam Wuruk [launched the third invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majapahit_invasion_of_Nan_Sarunai "Majapahit invasion of Nan Sarunai") of the [Dayak-Maʼanyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'anyan_people "Ma'anyan people") Kingdom of [Nan Sarunai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nan_Sarunai "Nan Sarunai") in southern [Borneo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borneo "Borneo"), at the time led by Raden Anyan ( [Datu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Datu "Datu") Tatuyan Wulau Miharaja Papangkat Amas).[&#8202;](#ref-back-jejakrekam.com_2018-0)[\[45\]](#refnote-jejakrekam.com_2018) According to the _[Hikayat Banjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hikayat_Banjar "Hikayat Banjar")_, a history of the kings of southern Borneo, this invasion was led by Ampu Jatmika from Kalingga in Kediri, along with his advisor Aria Megatsari, his general Tumenggung Tatah Jiwa, his minister Wiramartas, Patih Baras, Patih Basi, Patih Luhu, Patih Dulu, and his bodyguards Sang Panimba Segara, Sang Pembelah Batung, Sang Jampang Sasak, and Sang Pengeruntung 'Garuntung' Manau.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Munoz_2009-0)[\[46\]](#refnote-Munoz_2009) Multiple battles took place, with the first battle in April 1358;[&#8202;](#ref-back-Kusmartono_dan_Widianto_1998-0)[\[47\]](#refnote-Kusmartono_dan_Widianto_1998) killed Majapahit soldiers were burned in Tambak Wasi. Nansarunai captain Jamuhala was also killed in this battle. While prince Jarang and prince Idong hid in Man near Tabalong-kiwa river. Nansarunai soldiers were concentrated in Pulau Kadap before the second battle happened in December 1362. Casualties from this second battle were buried in _Tambak_ in Bayu Hinrang. In this war Raden Anyan was killed, speared by Mpu Nala, and buried in Banua Lawas.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Effrata_2021_pp._26–33-0)[\[48\]](#refnote-Effrata_2021_pp._26–33) In its place, Ampu Jatmika founded a Hindu kingdom state, Negara Dipa under Majapahit tributary, predecessor of [Banjar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banjar_Sultanate "Banjar Sultanate"). While surviving Javanese, Dayak, Madurese, and Bugis soldiers, sailors, metalsmiths of this war settled in Amuntai, Alabio, and Nagara.[&#8202;](#ref-back-jejakrekam.com_2018-1)[\[45\]](#refnote-jejakrekam.com_2018)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Effrata_2021_pp._26–33-1)[\[48\]](#refnote-Effrata_2021_pp._26–33) These invasions were recorded in [Dayak Ma'anyan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'anyan_people "Ma'anyan people") poetry as _Nansarunai Usak Jawa_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-56-0)[\[49\]](#refnote-56)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ahsan_2018-0)[\[50\]](#refnote-Ahsan_2018)

Along with launching naval and military expeditions, the expansion of the Majapahit Empire involved diplomacy and alliance. Hayam Wuruk decided, probably for political reasons, to take princess [Citra Rashmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyah_Pitaloka_Citraresmi "Dyah Pitaloka Citraresmi") (Dyah Pitaloka) of neighbouring [Sunda Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunda_Kingdom "Sunda Kingdom") as his [consort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_consort "Queen consort").[&#8202;](#ref-back-end-0)[\[51\]](#refnote-end):279 The Sundanese took this proposal as an alliance agreement. In 1357 the Sunda king and his royal family came to Majapahit to accompany and marry his daughter to Hayam Wuruk.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-4)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):239 However, Gajah Mada saw this event as an opportunity to demand Sunda's submission to Majapahit overlordship. The skirmish between the Sunda royal family and the Majapahit troops on Bubat square was inevitable. Despite courageous resistance, the royal family were overwhelmed and decimated. Almost the whole of the Sundanese royal party was killed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-59-0)[\[52\]](#refnote-59) Tradition mentioned that the heartbroken princess committed [suicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suicide "Suicide") to defend the honour of her country.[&#8202;](#ref-back-60-0)[\[53\]](#refnote-60) The [Battle of Bubat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bubat "Battle of Bubat"), or the Pasunda Bubat tragedy, became the main theme of _[Kidung Sunda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidung_Sunda "Kidung Sunda")_, also mentioned in _[Carita Parahyangan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carita_Parahyangan "Carita Parahyangan")_ and _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_, but it was never mentioned in _[Nagarakretagama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_.

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Prasasti_Gajah_Mada.jpg/250px-Prasasti_Gajah_Mada.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Prasasti_Gajah_Mada.jpg) [Gajah Mada inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada_inscription "Gajah Mada inscription"), dated 1273 Śaka (1351 AD), mentioned about a sacred caitya building dedicated by Gajah Mada for the late King [Kertanegara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kertanegara_of_Singhasari "Kertanegara of Singhasari") of Singhasari.

The _Nagarakretagama_, written in 1365, depicts a sophisticated court with refined taste in art and literature and a complex system of religious rituals. The poet describes Majapahit as the centre of a huge [mandala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandala "Mandala") extending from [New Guinea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Guinea "New Guinea") and [Maluku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maluku_Islands "Maluku Islands") to [Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumatra "Sumatra") and the [Malay Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_Peninsula "Malay Peninsula"). Local traditions in many parts of Indonesia retain accounts of 14th-century Majapahit's power in more or less [legendary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legend "Legend") form. The direct administration of Majapahit did not extend beyond [east Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Java "East Java") and [Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali "Bali"), but challenges to Majapahit's claim to overlordship in outer islands drew forceful responses.[&#8202;](#ref-back-miksic-0)[\[54\]](#refnote-miksic):106

To revive the fortune of [Malayu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melayu_Kingdom "Melayu Kingdom") in Sumatra, in the 1370s, a Malay ruler of [Palembang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palembang "Palembang") sent an envoy to the court of [the first emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongwu_Emperor "Hongwu Emperor") of the newly established [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty "Ming dynasty"). He invited China to resume the tributary system, just like [Srivijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srivijaya "Srivijaya") did several centuries earlier. Learning this diplomatic manoeuvre, immediately King Hayam Wuruk sent an envoy to Nanking, convinced the emperor that Malayu was their vassal, and was not an independent country.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Britannica-Majapahit-1)[\[10\]](#refnote-Britannica-Majapahit) Subsequently, in 1377,[&#8202;](#ref-back-63-0)[\[note 8\]](#refnote-63) a few years after the death of Gajah Mada, Majapahit sent a punitive naval attack against a rebellion in Palembang,[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-4)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):19 contributing to the end of the Srivijayan successor kingdom. Besides Gajah Mada, Another famous military leader was [Adityawarman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adityawarman "Adityawarman"), known for his chronicle in [Minangkabau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minangkabau_people "Minangkabau people").[&#8202;](#ref-back-64-0)[\[56\]](#refnote-64)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/eb/Cetbang_Majapahit_of_1470-1478%2C_collection_of_The_Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art.jpg/500px-Cetbang_Majapahit_of_1470-1478%2C_collection_of_The_Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cetbang_Majapahit_of_1470-1478,_collection_of_The_Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art.jpg) Bronze cannon, called [cetbang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cetbang "Cetbang"), [Metropolitan Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art "Metropolitan Museum of Art"), New York, from c. 1470–1478 Majapahit. Note the _[Surya Majapahit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya_Majapahit "Surya Majapahit")_ emblem on the bronze cannon.

The nature of the Majapahit empire and its extent is subject to debate. It may have had limited or entirely notional influence over some of the [tributary states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vassal "Vassal"), including Sumatra, the Malay Peninsula, [Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalimantan "Kalimantan"), and eastern Indonesia, over which authority was claimed in the _Nagarakretagama_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-atlas-0)[\[57\]](#refnote-atlas) Geographical and economic constraints suggest that rather than a regular centralised authority, the outer states were most likely to have been connected mainly by trade connections, which were probably a royal monopoly.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-5)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):19 It also claimed relationships with [Champa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champa "Champa"), [Cambodia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodia "Cambodia"), [Siam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand "Thailand"), southern Burma, and Vietnam, and even sent missions to China.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-6)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):19 Although the Majapahit rulers extended their power over other islands and destroyed neighbouring kingdoms, their focus seems to have been on controlling and gaining a larger share of the commercial trade that passed through the archipelago.

About the time Majapahit was founded, [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Indonesia "Islam in Indonesia") traders and [proselytisers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proselytise "Proselytise") began entering the area. The Troloyo/Tralaya tomb, a remnant of Islamic cemetery compound was discovered within the Trowulan area, the royal capital of Majapahit. Experts suggest that the cemetery was used between 1368 and 1611 AD, which means Muslim traders had resided in the capital as early as the mid-14th century during the reign of Hayam Wuruk.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Adrisijanti-0)[\[58\]](#refnote-Adrisijanti):185,196 Two Muslim tombstones in Troloyo were dated from the 14th century (1368 AD, 1376 AD). The close proximity of the site with the _kraton_ means there were Muslim people in close relation with the court.[&#8202;](#ref-back-67-0)[\[59\]](#refnote-67)

### Decline

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Decline")\]

Following [Hayam Wuruk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hayam_Wuruk "Hayam Wuruk")'s death in 1389, Majapahit entered a period of decline with conflict over succession.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-5)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):241 Hayam Wuruk was officially succeeded by his nephew and son-in-law, Prince [Vikramavardhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikramavardhana "Vikramavardhana"), who was married to Princess Kusumawardhani, Hayam Wuruk's only child by his primary wife. However, Hayam Wuruk had a son from a concubine, Bhre Wirabhumi, who also claimed the throne, eventually leading to the [Regreg Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regreg_War "Regreg War") from 1405 to 1406.

The identity and full name of Bhre Wirabhumi is unclear; "_Bhre_" simply means " [duke or duchess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duke "Duke")", and "Wirabhumi" is an area corresponding to the [Blambangan Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blambangan_Kingdom "Blambangan Kingdom"). According to the _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_, he was the son of Hayam Wuruk and a concubine, and was adopted as a foster son by Hayam Wuruk's aunt and uncle (as well as mother-in-law and father-in-law), Bhre Daha (Rajadewi) and Wijayarajasa. Later, Bhre Wirabhumi married Bhre Lasem Sang Alemu, the daughter of Hayam Wuruk's sister, Brhe Pajang.

According to the _[Nagarakretagama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_, the wife of Brhe Wirabhumi was Nagarawardhani, the daughter of Brhe Lasem (Indudewi). Indudewi was the daughter of Hayam Wuruk's aunt and uncle Bhre Daha (Rajadewi) and Wijayarajasa. The _Nagarakretagama_ is considered more authoritative than the _Pararaton_, since it was written during Brhe Wirabhumi's lifetime.

By the time of Hayam Wuruk's death, Majapahit had lost its grip on its vassal states on the northern coasts of Sumatra and the Malay Peninsula—the latter of which, according to Chinese sources, would become a tributary state of the [Ayutthaya Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayutthaya_Kingdom "Ayutthaya Kingdom") until the rise of the [Malacca Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malacca_Sultanate "Malacca Sultanate") as supported by the [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty "Ming dynasty").[&#8202;](#ref-back-notes_on_the_malay_archipelago-0)[\[60\]](#refnote-notes_on_the_malay_archipelago)

In 1299, the [Kingdom of Singapura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Singapura "Kingdom of Singapura") had been established, and following a [failed attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sack_of_Singapore#First_attempt_to_invade_Singapore "Sack of Singapore") to take Singapore in 1350, Majapahit [finally sacked and destroyed the Kingdom in 1398](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sack_of_Singapore "Sack of Singapore"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-69-0)[\[61\]](#refnote-69)[&#8202;](#ref-back-70-0)[\[62\]](#refnote-70)[&#8202;](#ref-back-71-0)[\[63\]](#refnote-71) after approximately a month-long siege by three hundred [jong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Djong "Djong") war ships and 200,000 men.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:10-0)[\[64\]](#refnote-:10)[&#8202;](#ref-back-73-0)[\[65\]](#refnote-73)[&#8202;](#ref-back-74-0)[\[66\]](#refnote-74)[&#8202;](#ref-back-75-0)[\[67\]](#refnote-75) The last king, [Parameswara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parameswara_(king) "Parameswara (king)"), fled to the west coast of the Malay Peninsula to establish the [Malacca Sultanate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malacca_Sultanate "Malacca Sultanate") in 1400.

#### Regreg War

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Regreg War")\]

Main article: [Regreg War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regreg_War "Regreg War")

A war of succession, called the [Regreg War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regreg_War "Regreg War"), is thought to have occurred from 1405 to 1406.[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-7)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):18 The war was fought between the _Kedhaton Kulon_ ( [western court in Trowulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan "Trowulan")), led by King [Vikramavardhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vikramavardhana "Vikramavardhana"), and the _Kedhaton Wetan_ ( [eastern court in the Blambangan Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blambangan_Kingdom "Blambangan Kingdom")), led by Bhre Wirabhumi. King Vikramavardhana was victorious, and Bhre Wirabhumi was caught and decapitated. However the civil war drained financial resources, exhausted the kingdom, and weakened Majapahit's grip on its outer vassals and colonies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Victor-0)[\[68\]](#refnote-Victor)

#### Wikramawardhana and Ming expedition

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Wikramawardhana and Ming expedition")\]

Further information: [Treasure voyages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treasure_voyages "Treasure voyages")

See also: [Battle of Palembang (1407)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Palembang_(1407) "Battle of Palembang (1407)")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Zheng_He.png/500px-Zheng_He.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Zheng_He.png) The route of the voyages of Zheng He's fleet, including Majapahit ports

During the reign of Wikramawardhana, a series of [Ming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty "Ming dynasty") armada [naval expeditions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treasure_voyages "Treasure voyages") led by [Zheng He](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheng_He "Zheng He"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-6)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):241–242 a [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim "Muslim") Chinese admiral, arrived in Java several times spanning the period from 1405 to 1433. These Chinese voyages visited numbers of ports in Asia as far as Africa, including Majapahit ports. It was said that Zheng He has paid a visit to the Majapahit court in Java.

These massive Chinese voyages were not merely a naval exploration, but also a [show of power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_projection "Power projection") and a display of geopolitical reach. The Chinese Ming dynasty had recently overthrown the Mongol-led Yuan dynasty, and was eager to establish their hegemony in the world, which changed the geopolitical balance in Asia.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TOI-Sanyal-0)[\[69\]](#refnote-TOI-Sanyal) The Chinese intervened in the politics of the southern seas by supporting Thais against the declining Khmer Empire, supporting and installing allied factions in India, [Sri Lanka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sri_Lanka "Sri Lanka") and other places in [Indian Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Ocean "Indian Ocean") coasts. However, perhaps the most significant Chinese intervention was its support for the newly established [Sultanate of Malacca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Malacca "Sultanate of Malacca") as a rival and counter-weight to the Majapahit influence of Java.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TOI-Sanyal-1)[\[69\]](#refnote-TOI-Sanyal)

Previously, Majapahit had succeeded in asserting its influence in Malacca strait by containing the aspiration of Malay polities in Sumatra and the Malay Peninsula to ever reach the geopolitical might like those of Srivijaya. The Hindu Majapahit was the most powerful maritime power in Southeast Asian seas that time and were opposed to Chinese expansion into their sphere of influence. The Ming's support for Malacca and the spread of Islam propagated by both Malacca and Zheng He's treasure fleet has weakened Majapahit maritime influence in Sumatra, which caused the northern part of the island to increasingly converting to Islam and gained independence from Majapahit, leaving [Indragiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indragiri_River "Indragiri River"), [Jambi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jambi "Jambi") and [Palembang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palembang "Palembang"), remnants of the old [Srivijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Srivijaya "Srivijaya"), the only suzerainty under Majapahit in Sumatra,[&#8202;](#ref-back-78-0)[\[note 9\]](#refnote-78) bordering [Pagaruyung Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagaruyung_Kingdom "Pagaruyung Kingdom") on the west and independent Muslim kingdoms on the north.

This Ming dynasty voyages are extremely important for Majapahit historiography, since Zheng He's translator [Ma Huan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma_Huan "Ma Huan") wrote _[Yingya Shenglan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yingya_Shenglan "Yingya Shenglan")_, a detailed description of Majapahit,[&#8202;](#ref-back-yingyai-1)[\[26\]](#refnote-yingyai) which provides valuable insight on the culture, customs, and also various social and economic aspects of Java during Majapahit period.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Chao-Wa-1)[\[27\]](#refnote-Chao-Wa)

The Chinese provided systematic support to Malacca, and its sultan made at least one trip to personally pay obeisance to the Ming emperor. Malacca actively encouraged the conversion to [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam") in the region, while the Ming fleet actively established Chinese-Malay Muslim community in coastal northern Java, thus created a permanent opposition to the Hindus of Java. By 1430, the expeditions had established Muslim Chinese, Arab and Malay communities in northern ports of Java such as [Semarang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semarang "Semarang"), [Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak,_Indonesia "Demak, Indonesia"), [Tuban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuban "Tuban"), and [Ampel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surabaya "Surabaya"); thus Islam began to gain a foothold on the northern coast of Java. Malacca prospered under Chinese Ming protection, while the Majapahit were steadily pushed back.[&#8202;](#ref-back-TOI-Sanyal-2)[\[69\]](#refnote-TOI-Sanyal)

#### Queen Suhita

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Queen Suhita")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Suhita.jpg/250px-Suhita.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Suhita.jpg) The mortuary deified portrait statue of Queen [Suhita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suhita "Suhita") (reign 1429–1447), discovered at Jebuk, Kalangbret, Tulungagung, East Java, [National Museum of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_of_Indonesia "National Museum of Indonesia")

Wikramawardhana ruled until 1429 and was succeeded by his daughter [Suhita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suhita "Suhita"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-7)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):242 who ruled from 1429 to 1447. She was the second child of Wikramawardhana by a concubine who was the daughter of Wirabhumi. She was married to future [Kelantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelantan "Kelantan") king Iskandar Shah or Kemas Jiwa in 1427. Both of them had a daughter together and he stayed with her in Java. [Kelantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelantan "Kelantan") was ruled by his brother, Sultan Sadik Muhammad Shah, until his death in 1429. This necessitated Kemas Jiwa to return and took the throne as Iskandar, where he declared Kelantan as Majapahit II in Mahligai.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Malaysiakini_2018-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-Malaysiakini_2018)\[ _[dubious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement "Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute")– [discuss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk:Majapahit#Dubious "Talk:Majapahit")_\] Although _Pararaton_ listed her husband as Bhra Hyang Parameswara Ratnapangkaja, which suggests she remarried after Kemas Jiwa returned.\[ _[dubious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement "Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute")– [discuss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk:Majapahit#Dubious "Talk:Majapahit")_\]

The reign of Suhita was the second time Majapahit was reigned by a queen regnant after her great-grandmother Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi. Her reign is immortalized in Javanese legend of [Damarwulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damarwulan "Damarwulan"), as it involves a maiden queen named Prabu Kenya in the story, and during Suhita's reign there was a war with [Blambangan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blambangan "Blambangan") as stated in the legend.[&#8202;](#ref-back-80-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-80)

In 1447, Suhita died and was succeeded by [Kertawijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kertawijaya "Kertawijaya"), her brother.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Coedes-8)[\[32\]](#refnote-Coedes):242 He ruled until 1451. After Kertawijaya died, [Bhre Pamotan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bhre_Pamotan?action=edit&redlink=1 "Bhre Pamotan (page does not exist)") became a king with formal name Rajasawardhana. He died in 1453. A three-year kingless period was possibly the result of a succession crisis. [Girisawardhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girisawardhana?action=edit&redlink=1 "Girisawardhana (page does not exist)"), son of Kertawijaya, came to power in 1456. He died in 1466 and was succeeded by Singhawikramawardhana.

#### The divide

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: The divide")\]

In 1468 Prince Kertabhumi rebelled against Singhawikramawardhana, promoting himself as the king of Majapahit. The deposed Singhawikramawardhana retreated upstream of Brantas River, moved the kingdom's capital further inland to [Daha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kediri_(city) "Kediri (city)") (the former capital of [Kediri kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kediri_(historical_kingdom) "Kediri (historical kingdom)")), effectively splitting Majapahit, under Bhre Kertabumi in Trowulan and Singhawikramawardhana in Daha. Singhawikramawardhana continued his rule until he was succeeded by his son [Girindrawardhana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girindrawardhana "Girindrawardhana") (Ranawijaya) in 1474.

In between this period of the dividing court of Majapahit, the kingdom found itself unable to control its western part of the already crumbling empire. The rising power of the [Sultanate of Malacca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Malacca "Sultanate of Malacca") began to gain effective control of the [Malacca Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strait_of_Malacca "Strait of Malacca") in the mid-15th century and expanding its influence to Sumatra. And amidst these events, Indragiri and Siantan, according to [Malay Annals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malay_Annals "Malay Annals") were given to Malacca as a dowry for the marriage of a Majapahit princess and the [sultan of Malacca](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansur_Shah_of_Malacca "Mansur Shah of Malacca"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-Leyden-0)[\[72\]](#refnote-Leyden) further weakening Majapahit's influence on the western part of the archipelago. Kertabhumi managed to stabilize this situation by allying with Muslim merchants, giving them trading rights on the north coast of Java, with [Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak_Regency "Demak Regency") as its centre and in return asked for their loyalty to Majapahit. This policy boosted the Majapahit treasury and power but weakened Hindu-Buddhism as its main religion because [Islamic proselytizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dawah "Dawah") spread faster, especially in Javanese coastal principalities. Hindu-Buddhist followers' grievances later paved the way for Ranawijaya to defeat Kertabumi.

Dates for the end of the Majapahit Empire range from 1478, traditionally described in _sinengkalan_ or _chandrasengkala_ ( [chronogram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chronogram "Chronogram")) _Sirna ilang kertaning bhumi_ that is correspond to 1400 [Śaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saka_era "Saka era"), to 1527.[&#8202;](#ref-back-82-0)[\[note 10\]](#refnote-82)[&#8202;](#ref-back-:11-0)[\[73\]](#refnote-:11):36 The year 1478 was the year of [Sudarma Wisuta war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sudarma_Wisuta_war "Sudarma Wisuta war"), when Ranawijaya's army under general Udara (who later became vice-regent) breached Trowulan defences and killed Kertabumi in his palace,[&#8202;](#ref-back-84-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-84)[&#8202;](#ref-back-85-0)[\[75\]](#refnote-85) but not the actual fall of Majapahit itself as a whole.

Demak sent reinforcements under [Sunan Ngudung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunan_Ngudung "Sunan Ngudung"), who later died in battle and was replaced by [Sunan Kudus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunan_Kudus "Sunan Kudus"), but they came too late to save Kertabumi although they managed to repel the Ranawijaya army. This event is mentioned in Trailokyapuri (Jiyu) and Petak inscription, where Ranawijaya claimed that he already defeated Kertabhumi and reunited Majapahit as one Kingdom.[&#8202;](#ref-back-SNI448-0)[\[76\]](#refnote-SNI448) Ranawijaya ruled from 1474 to 1498 with the formal name Girindrawardhana, with Udara as his vice-regent. This event led to the war between the [Sultanate of Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Demak "Sultanate of Demak") and [Daha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kediri_(historical_kingdom) "Kediri (historical kingdom)") since Demak rulers were descendants of Kertabhumi.\[ _[dubious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement "Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute")– [discuss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk:Majapahit#Dubious "Talk:Majapahit")_\]

During this period, [Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak_Sultanate "Demak Sultanate"), being the dominant ruler of the Javanese coastal lands and Java as a whole, seized the region of Jambi and Palembang in Sumatra from Majapahit.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:3-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-:3):154–155

#### Demak invasion and the fall of Majapahit

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: Demak invasion and the fall of Majapahit")\]

Main article: [Demak–Majapahit conflicts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak%E2%80%93Majapahit_conflicts "Demak–Majapahit conflicts")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/ce/Masjid_Agung_Demak.jpg/250px-Masjid_Agung_Demak.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Masjid_Agung_Demak.jpg) [Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak_Sultanate "Demak Sultanate") was the earliest Islamic polity in Java that replaced Majapahit.

In 1498, there was a turning point when Girindrawardhana was deposed by his vice-regent, Udara. After this coup, the war between Demak and Majapahit receded, since [Raden Patah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Patah "Raden Patah"), Sultan of Demak, left Majapahit alone like his father had done before, some source said Udara agreed to become a vassal of Demak, even marrying Raden Patah's youngest daughter.

Meanwhile, in the west, [Malacca was captured by Portuguese in 1511](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capture_of_Malacca_(1511) "Capture of Malacca (1511)"). The delicate balance between Demak and Majapahit ended when Udara, seeing an opportunity to eliminate Demak, asked for [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portuguese_Malacca "Portuguese Malacca") help in Malacca, forcing Demak to attack both Malacca and Majapahit under [Adipati Yunus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak_Sultanate#Adipati_Yunus "Demak Sultanate") to end this alliance.[&#8202;](#ref-back-90-0)[\[note 11\]](#refnote-90)

With the fall of Majapahit, crushed by Demak in 1527,[&#8202;](#ref-back-:11-1)[\[73\]](#refnote-:11):54–55 the Muslim emerging forces finally defeated the remnants of the Majapahit kingdom in the early 16th century;[&#8202;](#ref-back-91-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-91) and with the fall of Majapahit, a large number of courtiers, artisans, priests, and members of the royalty moved east to the island of [Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali "Bali"). The refugees fled to the east to avoid Demak retribution for their support for Ranawijaya against Kertabhumi.

Demak came under the leadership of Raden (later crowned as Sultan) Patah, who was acknowledged as the legitimate successor of Majapahit. According to Babad Tanah Jawi and Demak tradition, the source of Patah's legitimacy was because their first sultan, Raden Patah, was the son of Majapahit king Brawijaya V with a Chinese concubine. Another argument supports Demak as the successor of Majapahit; the rising Demak sultanate was easily accepted as the nominal regional ruler, as Demak was the former Majapahit vassal and located near the former Majapahit realm in eastern Java.

Demak established itself as the regional power and the first Islamic sultanate in Java. After the fall of Majapahit, the Hindu kingdoms in Java only remained in [Pasuruan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasuruan "Pasuruan"), [Panarukan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panarukan "Panarukan"), and [Blambangan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blambangan "Blambangan")[&#8202;](#ref-back-:18-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-:18):7 on the eastern edge and [Sunda Kingdom Pajajaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunda_Kingdom "Sunda Kingdom") in the western part. Gradually [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hindu "Hindu") communities began to retreat to the mountain ranges in East Java and also to the neighbouring island of [Bali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali "Bali"). A small enclave of [Hindu communities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenggerese_people "Tenggerese people") remain in the [Tengger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tengger_caldera "Tengger caldera") mountain range.

## Culture

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Culture")\]

The Majapahit capital of [Trowulan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trowulan "Trowulan") was grand and known for its great annual festivities. The main event of the administrative calendar took place on the first day of the month of [Caitra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaitra "Chaitra") (March–April), the first month of the lunar calendar, when representatives from all territories paying [tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax "Tax") or [tribute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribute "Tribute") to Majapahit came to the capital to pay court. Majapahit's territories were roughly divided into three types: the palace and its vicinity; the areas of east Java and Bali which were directly administered by officials appointed by the king; and the outer [dependencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dependent_territory "Dependent territory") which enjoyed substantial internal [autonomy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomy "Autonomy").[&#8202;](#ref-back-miksic-1)[\[54\]](#refnote-miksic):107[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ritualnetwork-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-Ritualnetwork)
