[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3e/Theatrical_Majapahit.jpg/250px-Theatrical_Majapahit.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Theatrical_Majapahit.jpg) Theatrical performance depicting the Mongol invasion of Java, performed by 150 students of [Indonesian Institute of the Arts, Yogyakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Institute_of_the_Arts,_Yogyakarta "Indonesian Institute of the Arts, Yogyakarta"). The history of Majapahit continues to inspire contemporary artists.

Celebrated as 'the golden era of the archipelago', the Majapahit empire has inspired many writers and artists (and continues to do so) to create their works based on this era or to describe and mention it. The impact of the Majapahit theme on popular culture can be seen in the following:

### Literature

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=39 "Edit section: Literature")\]

- _Sandyakalaning Majapahit_ (1933), or Twilight/Sunset in Majapahit is a historical romance that took place during the fall of Majapahit empire, written by [Sanusi Pane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanusi_Pane "Sanusi Pane").
- _Panji Koming_ (since 1979), a weekly [comic strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comic_strip "Comic strip") by Dwi Koendoro published in the Sunday edition of [Kompas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kompas "Kompas"), telling the everyday life of Panji Koming, a common Majapahit citizen. Although it took place in the Majapahit era, the comic strip serves as witty [satire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satire "Satire") and [criticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism "Criticism") of modern Indonesian society. From a political, social, cultural and current point of view, Indonesia is described as the ' [reincarnation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reincarnation "Reincarnation")' of the Majapahit empire. The current Indonesian president is often portrayed as a Majapahit monarch or prime minister.
- _Senopati Pamungkas_ (1986, reprinted in 2003), a martial art-historical epic novel by Arswendo Atmowiloto. It takes place in the late Singhasari period and formation of Majapahit. This novel describes the sagas, royal intrigues, and romance of the formation of the kingdom as well as the adventure of the main character, a commoner named Upasara Wulung and his forbidden love affair with princess Gayatri Rajapatni, whom later becomes the consort of [Raden Wijaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raden_Wijaya "Raden Wijaya"), the first king of Majapahit.
- _Imperium Majapahit_, a comic book series by Jan Mintaraga, published by Elexmedia Komputindo. This series tells the history of Majapahit from its formation until the decline.
- _[Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada_(book)?action=edit&redlink=1 "Gajah Mada (book) (page does not exist)")_, a [pentalogy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentalogy "Pentalogy") written by [Langit Kresna Hariadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Langit_Kresna_Hariadi?action=edit&redlink=1 "Langit Kresna Hariadi (page does not exist)") depicting a fictionalised detail of [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada")'s life from the [Kuti rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada#Rise_to_power "Gajah Mada") up to the [Bubat War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada#The_Bubat_Incident "Gajah Mada").
- _[Dyah Pitaloka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyah_Pitaloka?action=edit&redlink=1 "Dyah Pitaloka (page does not exist)")_ (2007), a novel written by Hermawan Aksan, fictionalising the detailed life story of Sundanese princess [Dyah Pitaloka Citraresmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyah_Pitaloka_Citraresmi "Dyah Pitaloka Citraresmi") set around the [Bubat War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada#The_Bubat_Incident "Gajah Mada"). The novel virtually takes the same context and was inspired by the [Kidung Sundayana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidung_Sundayana "Kidung Sundayana").
- _Jung Jawa_ (2009), an anthology of short stories written by Rendra Fatrisna Kurniawan, imagining the life of the Nusantara people, published by Babel Publishing.

### Film and Radio

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=40 "Edit section: Film and Radio")\]

- _Saur Sepuh_ (1987–1991), a radio drama and film by Niki Kosasih. Begun as a popular radio drama program in the late 1980s, Saur Sepuh is based on 15th-century Java, centred around the story about a fictional hero named Brama Kumbara, the king of Madangkara, a fictional kingdom neighbour of the [Pajajaran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Sunda "Kingdom of Sunda"). In several stories the Paregreg war is described, that is to say, the civil war of Majapahit between Wikramawardhana and Bhre Wirabhumi. This part has been made into a single feature film entitled 'Saur Sepuh' as well.
- _Tutur Tinular_, a radio drama and film by S Tidjab. Tutur Tinular is a [martial art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_art "Martial art") historical epic fictional story with the Majapahit era serving as the background of the story. The story also involved a romance between the hero named Arya Kamandanu and his Chinese lover Mei Shin.
- _Wali Songo_, the film tells the story of [nine Muslim saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wali_Sanga "Wali Sanga") ('wali') who spread [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam "Islam") to Java. The story took place near the end of the Majapahit era and the formation of [Demak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demak_Sultanate "Demak Sultanate"). It describes the decaying Majapahit empire where royals are fighting each other for power while the commoners suffer.
- _[Puteri Gunung Ledang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puteri_Gunung_Ledang_(film) "Puteri Gunung Ledang (film)")_ (2004), a [Malaysian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia "Malaysia") epic film based on [a traditional Malay legend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legend_of_Puteri_Gunung_Ledang "Legend of Puteri Gunung Ledang"). This film recounts the love story between Gusti Putri Retno Dumilah, a Majapahit princess, and [Hang Tuah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hang_Tuah "Hang Tuah"), a [Malaccan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaccan_Sultanate "Malaccan Sultanate") admiral.

### Video game

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=41 "Edit section: Video game")\]

- _[Civilization 5: Brave New World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilization_5:_Brave_New_World "Civilization 5: Brave New World") (2013)_, where [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada") appeared as one of the leaders of a great civilisation in the second expansion of the [Civilization 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilization_5 "Civilization 5") game. He is the leader of the Indonesian civilisation, with the emblem of the Indonesian empire being the [Surya Majapahit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya_Majapahit "Surya Majapahit"), although in the game the empire is known as the "Indonesian empire" instead.[&#8202;](#ref-back-217-0)[\[195\]](#refnote-217)
- Later, in [_Civilization 6 (2016),_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilization_VI "Civilization VI") _a_ downloadable content has one of its rulers, [Dyah Gitarja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tribhuwana_Wijayatunggadewi "Tribhuwana Wijayatunggadewi") (referred as Gitarja in-game) as the leader of the Indonesian civilization, with the emblem being a simpler version of the Surya Majapahit. The unique unit of Indonesian civilization is the _jong_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-218-0)[\[196\]](#refnote-218)
- On the _Rise of the Rajas_ expansion pack from _[Age of Empires II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Empires_II "Age of Empires II")_, [Gajah Mada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gajah_Mada "Gajah Mada") appears in a campaign detailing his rise and then fall after the Pasunda Bubat tragedy. He also made appearance in the _[Age of Empires II: Definitive Edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Empires_II:_Definitive_Edition "Age of Empires II: Definitive Edition")_.
- A unit called Cetbang Cannon is available for Indonesia, a revolutionary nation available for the Dutch and Portuguese civilization in _[Age of Empires III: Definitive Edition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Empires_III:_Definitive_Edition "Age of Empires III: Definitive Edition")_.
- Majapahit is featured as a civilization in _[Civilization VII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilization_VII "Civilization VII")_. They appear during the exploration period of the game.[&#8202;](#ref-back-219-0)[\[197\]](#refnote-219)

## Notes

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Majapahit&action=edit&section=43 "Edit section: Notes")\]

01. [Surya Majapahit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya_Majapahit "Surya Majapahit") (the Sun of Majapahit) is the emblem commonly found in Majapahit ruins. It served as the symbol of the Majapahit empire.
02. [&#8202;](#refnote-2)[↑](#ref-back-2-0) "Jump up")[Surya Majapahit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surya_Majapahit "Surya Majapahit") (the Sun of Majapahit) is the emblem commonly found in Majapahit ruins. It served as the symbol of the Majapahit empire.
03. [&#8202;](#refnote-4)[↑](#ref-back-4-0) "Jump up")Later period only
04. [&#8202;](#refnote-6)[↑](#ref-back-6-0) "Jump up")Although officially recognized as _[Maharaja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maharaja "Maharaja")_, Girindrawardhana did not have a major role in his government. Most of the governmental duties were carried out by Patih Udara, who mainly led the remnants of Majapahit in Daha before the collapse of the empire.
05. [&#8202;](#refnote-9)[↑](#ref-back-9-0) "Jump up")Some Javanese court literature use this [Sanskrit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanskrit "Sanskrit")-derived name, which bears the same meaning ("bitter gourd") as "Majapahit" does in Old Javanese, for example in the _[Deśavarṇana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_ (canto 1 stanza 2) and _Kidung Harsawijaya_ (canto 4 stanza 66). It is sometimes also written backwards as Tiktawilwa, for example in the _Deśavarṇana_ (canto 18 stanza 4). However it is still more widely known by its Old Javanese name, as recorded in Javanese and Balinese manuscripts, as well as in the _hikayat_ s of Aceh, Banjar, Malay, Palembang, etc.
06. [&#8202;](#refnote-31)[↑](#ref-back-31-0) "Jump up")[&#8202;](#ref-back-30-0)[\[25\]](#refnote-30) cited in[&#8202;](#ref-back-ricklefs-14)[\[11\]](#refnote-ricklefs):18,311
07. [&#8202;](#refnote-49)[↑](#ref-back-49-0) "Jump up")Mainly _[Nagarakretagama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nagarakretagama "Nagarakretagama")_, _[Pararaton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pararaton "Pararaton")_, _[Suma Oriental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom%C3%A9_Pires#Suma_Oriental "Tomé Pires")_, and [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty "Ming dynasty") records
08. [&#8202;](#refnote-63)[↑](#ref-back-63-0) "Jump up")According to the Chinese sources, the event took place in 1376 or 1377, according to the Javanese about 1361. See[&#8202;](#ref-back-62-0)[\[55\]](#refnote-62)
09. [&#8202;](#refnote-78)[↑](#ref-back-78-0) "Jump up") According to the History of Ming, only these areas in Sumatra were still mentioned as under Javanese / Majapahit supremacy at the time. Read[&#8202;](#ref-back-notes_on_the_malay_archipelago-2)[\[60\]](#refnote-notes_on_the_malay_archipelago)
10. [&#8202;](#refnote-82)[↑](#ref-back-82-0) "Jump up")The year 1400 Śaka is marked among Javanese today with _[candrasengkala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chronogram "Chronogram")_ " _sirna ilang kertaning bumi_" (the wealth of earth disappeared and diminished) ( _sirna_ = 0, _ilang_ = 0, _kerta_ = 4, _bumi_ = 1).
11. [&#8202;](#refnote-90)[↑](#ref-back-90-0) "Jump up")V.[&#8202;](#ref-back-88-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-88) Another paper noted that the reasons for the attacks Demak (led by Adipati Yunus) to Majapahit (Girindrawardhana period) are a backlash against Girindrawardhana who had defeated Adipati Yunus' grandfather Prabu Bhre Kertabumi (Prabu Brawijaya V).[&#8202;](#ref-back-89-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-89)
12. [&#8202;](#refnote-94)[↑](#ref-back-94-0) "Jump up")Odoric of Pordenone mentioned that the King of Java ruled over "seven crowned kings", possibly refer to _Bhattara Saptaprabhu_ or seven Bhattara or Bhres (Dukes/Duchess), which is seven influential elders that rules over seven nagara or regional kingdoms, corresponds to Majapahit provinces in East and Central Java; i.e. Kahuripan, Daha, Tumapel, Wengker, Lasem, Pajang, and Mataram.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Ritualnetwork-1)[\[82\]](#refnote-Ritualnetwork)
13. [&#8202;](#refnote-95)[↑](#ref-back-95-0) "Jump up")The Javanese victory on repelling the [Mongol forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasion_of_Java "Mongol invasion of Java") during the formation of Majapahit seems to be popular news being retold in Java over generations.
14. [&#8202;](#refnote-98)[↑](#ref-back-98-0) "Jump up")In the same fashion as traditional costumes of how [Balinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balinese_people "Balinese people") wore unstitched [sarong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarong "Sarong") around their waist or how [Javanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Javanese_people "Javanese people") wore _kain_ [batik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batik "Batik") around their waist
15. [&#8202;](#refnote-99)[↑](#ref-back-99-0) "Jump up")One's head is considered sacred since it is where the soul resides, beliefs and customs still practised in modern Indonesia.
16. [&#8202;](#refnote-118)[↑](#ref-back-118-0) "Jump up")Bhre Wirabhumi is actually the title: the Duke of Wirabhumi (Blambangan), the real name is unknown. He is referred to as Bhre Wirabhumi in Pararaton. He married Nagawardhani, the king's niece.
17. [&#8202;](#refnote-jointheirs)[Jump up to: 1](#ref-back-jointheirs-0) [2](#ref-back-jointheirs-1)Kusumawardhani (king's daughter) married Wikramawardhana (king's nephew), the couple became joint-heirs.
18. [&#8202;](#refnote-141)[↑](#ref-back-141-0) "Jump up")A type of scatter bullet – when shot it spews fire, splinters and bullets, and can also be arrows. The characteristic of this projectile is that the bullet does not cover the entire bore of the barrel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-140-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-140)
19. [&#8202;](#refnote-148)[↑](#ref-back-148-0) "Jump up")_Dadap_ has 2 meanings: In Indonesian language, it refers to round shield made of leather or rattan,[&#8202;](#ref-back-145-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-145) while in old Javanese it refers to a long, narrow parrying shield.[&#8202;](#ref-back-146-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-146) _Dadap_ in Java seems to refer to a long shield which is quite heavy, probably with protruding ends.[&#8202;](#ref-back-147-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-147)
20. [&#8202;](#refnote-150)[↑](#ref-back-150-0) "Jump up")For the meaning of _sodok_, see[&#8202;](#ref-back-149-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-149)
21. [&#8202;](#refnote-152)[↑](#ref-back-152-0) "Jump up")_Rampuk_ likely derived from Old Javanese _rampog_ and _ngrampog_, which means "to attack in great numbers". Old Javanese _watang parampogan_ means a pike used in the _parampogan_, that is, tiger spearing ( _[rampokan macan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rampokan "Rampokan")_).[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-151)
22. [&#8202;](#refnote-157)[↑](#ref-back-157-0) "Jump up")_Kawaca_ has two meaning. The first one is a [shirt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shirt "Shirt") or [dress shirt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dress_shirt "Dress shirt") (Indonesian: _kemeja_) worn by clergy, the other means armor. See[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTENugroho2011386-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTENugroho2011386)

[_Xingcha Shenglan (星槎勝覽, The Overall Survey of the Star Raft)_](https://ctext.org/wiki.pl?if=en&chapter=436710). pp.25–26\. 25 爪哇國（自占城起程，順風二十晝夜可至其國）26 古名闍婆，地廣人稠，實甲兵器械，乃為東洋諸番之衝要。舊傳鬼子魔天，正於此地，與一罔象青面紅身赤髮相合。凡生子百餘，常食啖人血肉。佛書所云鬼國，其中只此地也。人被啖幾盡，忽一曰雷震石裂，中坐一人，眾稱異之，遂為國主，即領兵驅逐罔象，而不為害。後複生齒而安業，乃至今國之移文，後書一千三百七十六年。考之肇啟漢初，傳至我宣德七年。
